# Fidelity register

Generated by `tools/fidelity_register.py`; edit the source annotations or declarations, then regenerate.

The acceptance gate is **zero unresolved assumptions in required original behavior**. All APPROX IDs below remain unresolved while their labels exist. Tags describing developer-only behavior or presentation conventions also remain listed; this inventory does not judge their relevance or downgrade them.

EXT labels identify deliberate extensions. DATA labels identify claimed original-data provenance. BIN labels cite the original executable logic a rule was traced to (`STP-PPC` = Mac PowerPC Sim Theme Park, `TPI-EXE` = Theme Park Inc Game.exe, `TPW-EXE` = Windows Sim Theme Park World v2.0 tp.exe; see docs/reverse/FINDINGS.md). None of these labels proves semantic correctness or cancels an APPROX label at the same site. Acceptance also requires evidence for required behavior, assets, inputs and outputs; a zero tag count alone cannot satisfy that gate.

This is a tag inventory, not enforcement of all semantics. It does not detect unlabelled magic numbers, formulas, logic, omitted features or false provenance claims. It scans whole-line C# // and /// comments under source, excluding bin/obj; inline trailing comments, block comments and other languages are outside its scope. ID templates and runtime log string templates are not code-site annotations.

```sh
python3 tools/fidelity_register.py --write
python3 tools/fidelity_register.py --check
python3 -m unittest discover -s tools -p 'test_fidelity_register.py' -v
```

Current inventory: **286 unresolved unique APPROX IDs**, 378 APPROX occurrences, 295 EXT occurrences, 235 DATA occurrences and 1461 BIN occurrences.

Of these, 268 IDs belong to the existing original-fidelity areas (required-behavior scope remains unadjudicated), and 18 ONLINE IDs record uncertainty inside the allowed OpenTPW online extension. The extension scope does not hide, resolve or subtract those APPROX IDs from the total.

CI checks annotation/declaration consistency and document freshness only. It does not fail the build based on the unresolved count and does not establish the original-fidelity release gate.

Repeated source occurrences of one ID are allowed and listed separately. Duplicate register declarations, malformed/missing ID syntax, unregistered IDs and declarations without source annotations fail the check. Register declarations are read from the 23 configured C# registers (current one-entry-per-line tuple/new syntax) and the explicit three-column Approximation register table in docs/ONLINE.md; these are bounded lexical readers, not general C# or Markdown parsers. New registers must be explicitly configured. The check also compares the complete regenerated document against this file.

| COAST-029 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | COAST-STATION: the station is the original's three nodes at the model's dummies (a StdPylon in the cell beyond '&lt;', a pylon-less node on the middle dummy, a StdPylon beyond '&gt;', the end node on the first one's knot); what is not traced: a station whose dummies lie below the StdPylon's h0 (Ghosta Coasta 2.04 against 4, Scatty Batty 3.5 against 5, Escape Velocity 12.04 against 30, Candy Coaster 7.09 against 10) stands on pylons raised to h0 by the loft clamp, so S0 and S2 sit above the middle knot, which the original's pylon pose and terrain height may not do; the middle knot takes fTrackHeightAdjust (the Big Dripper's -1.5) like the pylon knots and the rating pass re-derives the platform's winch marks | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterApproximations.cs:25` |

| ECON-001 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | OpenTPW's fixed 60 Hz clock is sampled into 248 ms park turns (4.03 a second at normal speed) without the original's 31 ms substeps; under load the two differ: OpenTPW runs at most 16 ticks (266.7 ms) a frame for the whole simulation and drops the rest, where the original's world substeps catch up a backlog of up to 2000 ms but at most three park turns run a frame (further due turns in that frame are skipped, not deferred) | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/Economy/EconomyApproximations.cs:12` |
| ECON-007 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | a repaid loan is offered again at once and every offer not taken is listed, without a credit test: the original clears the bought flag at the last instalment (0x4D0370) and lists an offer on the Available tab (0x49FA00) only when it is not bought and its stored test passed, amount ≤ net worth (0x4C7DB0, the park value with its cell points, ECON-026) − the remaining instalments of the bought loans (0x4D06B0), stored whenever the list is built (the dialog opens, a tab is selected); taking a loan needs only the bought flag clear (0x4D0750) | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/Economy/EconomyApproximations.cs:13` |
| ECON-014 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | staff vitals and strikes follow the traced routines (tp.exe month gate 0x508E70, escalation 0x508F70, grievance 0x509360; the month message comes from the calendar update 0x4F8260 with no game-type test, and the age stamp 0x4F8800 counts floor(turn × 3750) seconds from world turn 0, both as here); without a staff world (a headless economy) they skip the state machine: walking members drain every turn, working means a job in progress, resting and striking need no walk, idle members are not told apart and the rest search runs every turn; the month gate skips a month while any guest is in the park by state, where the original counts the guests standing on a cell of type 0, 1, 3, 9 or 10 in a census reused for up to 23 turns (0x4C7FA0); the handymen's litter share counts the live map (ECON-020); the forced-strike word (zero at creation 0x5089FE, written only by the save 0x508BB0) is not imported (SAVE-009) | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/Economy/EconomyApproximations.cs:14` |
| ECON-017 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | a new park's research table is in info-id order, where the original fills it in its object loader's order (0x5031A0 over 0x412E60 / 0x412E70, no sort on that path); the cursor steps with the arrows (0x4AA0C0 → 0x5034F0 / 0x5036A0) and moves on after a completed project (0x4AAEC0) to the next eligible project in table order with wrap, as the original's picker does, but the original appends an object's next level project to the end of the table only when the level below completes (0x503D40 → 0x504580 at 0x503D8C), where OpenTPW lists every level right after its base project, so after a level completes the two cursors can land on different projects; the picker's second test for an add-on whose target has a golden-ticket cost (+0xC4 &gt; 0 → 0x4D4B70 over the set at 0xF7C58C) is not modelled; and the original marks an object researched at the start when its first research cost (+0x180) is 0, in any group, where OpenTPW does so for every group-0 item; the shipped Jungle lab lists its projects in another order (rides 1140, 1150, 1108, …; features 1414 before 1400), which an imported park follows for its current projects only | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/Economy/EconomyApproximations.cs:15` |
| ECON-020 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | litter lies on map cells (one item per cell, dropped by guests' full litter meters, vomit and bins they cannot reach) with the original's sprite offset (0x004D93B0), but the drop's reseed of the original's world generator with the cell id (0x004D94E2) is not followed by OpenTPW's separate streams (DET-014), and the handymen's strike share (0x00509360) and the guests' thought pick (0x004FC8A0) count the live map, where the analyser recounts a 4 × 4 block's littered cells (0x004C8BA0) and path cells (0x004C8AE0) only when (turn &gt;&gt; 2) − (stamp &gt;&gt; 2) exceeds the reader's age (1 for the strike, 4 for the thought), so the counts can be 7 or 19 turns old | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/Economy/EconomyApproximations.cs:16` |
| ECON-022 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | all staff in a level walk and work as the original's state machines (WANDER-plan §4, StaffWorldSimulation), with the shared walks to a rest area and to the strike area, but they step without the steering the original's shared walk step applies to staff as to guests (0x004FA2A0 → 0x0050F3B0 over the global list 0x007CF078); a striker walks out along arrival lane A (OpenTPW's path grid ends at the park entrance) and is not sent after a prankster until he is back on the paths; a resting member stays on the map, where the original frees his model on arrival (0x005054D0 state 3 → 0x004D4170) and recreates it when the rest ends (0x00506D10); a sold rest area ends the walk to it or the rest in it, where the original (removal message 10 from 0x004DD0A0, handled by 0x00504C70) also sends a resting member to the nearest other rest area; a toilet claim ends when the walk to it fails, and a mechanic's claim when his walk fails or the ride no longer needs him, where the original's claim (0x004E0220) lapses only once 100 turns have passed and the claimant no longer targets the object; a toilet's, bin's or rest area's access cell is its join or entrance cell or first path neighbour; a mechanic's finder scans the objects from the first, where the original keeps one cursor on the mechanic HQ (game +0x1DA71A, 0x004DAA90) that starts each search one object after the previous start, so the search after one that started at the last object finds no ride; a repair does not wait for a still-broken ride's fix animation (0x004DA830, 0x004E0410); an entertainer's performance has no animation (the original draws one, 0x004D46D0) and the guests do not turn to watch; the guard list runs in hire order, where the original's (game +0x1DA744, next +0x210) is head-inserted at hire (0x004D5DE0 → 0x00519AA0), so the newest guard wins a tie; a prank reaches the guards at once; a picked-up entertainer's or guard's row stays on the cell he was lifted from; a guard carries a caught guest to the park entrance when the ticket booth is no path cell, where the original routes only to the booth gate cell, twice (0x004D6790 → 0x004D8610), and otherwise sets the guest down (0x004FEB50); a headless economy without a world keeps its own handyman dispatch: the first unclaimed littered cell in the finder's order, park-wide, else the dirtiest toilet, timed from the dispatch | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/Economy/EconomyApproximations.cs:17` |
| ECON-023 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | the ride's state is derived from the economy (breakdown flag, pending upgrade) and the script's VAR_BROKEN instead of a state word: an economy breakdown (a life or repair byte of 0, relief objects exempt) marks the ride broken down at once, where the original only writes VAR_BREAKSTAT = 1 (0x004E0CA5 / 0x004E0CFA) and leaves it in state 0 until the script sets VAR_BROKEN (state 1, or 4 with a life byte of 0: 0x004E14E0); a breakdown that only the script reports gets no mechanic, where the original's state 1 gets one (0x004DAA90) and the repair waits for VAR_BROKEN to clear (0x004DA830); VAR_WORN is not written (the wear step 0x004DF670 sets it below a repair byte of 20, the update for relief objects below 25 at 0x004E0D16); only rides are updated, where the original updates every choosable object (state 0 at 0x004DB4FB): the shipped toilets are choosable with WearRate 0, so only their use (0x004E2440, modelled) wears them; state 2 comes only from the upgrade request 0x004E0050 (buy handler 0x004AF480) | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/Economy/EconomyApproximations.cs:18` |

| ONLINE-001 | OpenTPW online extension | Word filtering uses case-insensitive substring matches in space-padded text; entry spaces act as boundaries; allowed substrings exempt matches; hit characters except spaces become asterisks. | Original word-filter code or observed original filtering behavior; the source comment says the encrypted TP.ICD implementation is unreadable. | `docs/ONLINE.md:348` |

| ONLINE-004 | OpenTPW online extension | Buddy response strings 111–113 mean online, offline and removed. | Localized variants or the original response-code table. | `docs/ONLINE.md:350` |
| ONLINE-005 | OpenTPW online extension | Chat command semantics follow command words, response strings and weachatr.dll export names; say/emote/shout reach a room, wshout/ushout all rooms; hearing has no positional effect; blackmark creates a moderation report. | Original chat server behavior, including command reach, hearing range and blackmark handling. | `docs/ONLINE.md:351` |
| ONLINE-006 | OpenTPW online extension | A visitor already inside a park must leave it before visiting another, inferred from CHAT_COMMANDS string 127. | Original park-visit transition behavior or an original chat/runtime trace. | `docs/ONLINE.md:352` |
| ONLINE-007 | OpenTPW online extension | The globe camera's rates, steps, damping and radius ease are applied per tick at 30 ticks per second, and the angles move by velocity × ticks. | The original (tp.exe 0x5D5C50 → 0x5DC860) runs once a frame with no fixed rate: it damps the velocities once a frame, then moves the angles by velocity × dt and eases the radius by 0.3 × dt, dt = min(elapsed ms, 500) × 0.01. Still unknown: its frame rate on the online screens and how often a held panel arrow repeats its step (0x4C53F0, events 0x10008/0x10009). | `docs/ONLINE.md:353` |
| ONLINE-009 | OpenTPW online extension | Edge and right-button steering add rate × offset × step per tick to the turn velocity. | The original (0x5DC860) adds, once a frame and without the step, rate × 50 × the cursor's depth into the 1 % edge band (at most rate / 2) or rate × the right button's offset from the centre; these need its per-frame integration (ONLINE-007), so the frame rate is still unknown. | `docs/ONLINE.md:354` |
| ONLINE-010 | OpenTPW online extension | The hovered city's name and park count are drawn at the top right in the labels' rectangles (tp.exe table 0x7581A0: 93201 (1477,90)-(2048,240), 93202 (1627,275)-(2048,395)), white and centred, without the &#96;hilight&#96; model their table entries bind. | Whether a label draws that model (highlight.MD2) behind its text. | `docs/ONLINE.md:355` |
| ONLINE-012 | OpenTPW online extension | Edge steering starts once the cursor has moved on the globe screen. | The original has no per-screen gate: it reads the online front end's last mouse-move position (0x5D5DD0), the canvas centre until the first move after the front end starts (0x5D5770), so it steers at once when the cursor already rests at an edge (for example back from a city lobby). Still unknown: an OpenTPW home for that move history across the online screens. | `docs/ONLINE.md:356` |
| ONLINE-013 | OpenTPW online extension | Pins stand on the mesh surface (radius 119.7) at the decoded (s, s, p) scale of their root node. | The original puts the pin's root node at the city record's position vector (0x5DC3E0 → 0x5D8190), which the city list from weacityr.dll fills; its radius is not decoded (a city list from the original service or a capture of its globe). | `docs/ONLINE.md:357` |
| ONLINE-017 | OpenTPW online extension | The publish globe shows UITEXT 419 in the city name label while no city is hovered. | Its panel is read (tp.exe 0x5DD910 → 0x4C4FB0(1): the worldlob table without the satellites, Find Parks and back, with the city labels); neither tp.exe nor the Mac publish globe code loads UITEXT 419 as an immediate, so where it showed is unknown. | `docs/ONLINE.md:358` |
| ONLINE-019 | OpenTPW online extension | A chat park is shown as a flat overhead view in which each player is a marker that the server's relayed movement moves; the player walks their own marker by clicking (one walk speed; the original's double-click teleport is not separated). | The original chat park's three-dimensional scene, avatar model and animation (manual pp. 63-68) are not decoded. | `docs/ONLINE.md:359` |
| ONLINE-020 | OpenTPW online extension | The park details' eighth label row shows the players currently in the park's chat room (UITEXT 273). | The original's last row showed the Instant action mode flag (UITEXT 282), which the package format does not record. | `docs/ONLINE.md:361` |
| ONLINE-021 | OpenTPW online extension | The chat overlay's action buttons are a fixed top bar (vote, earmuffs, postcards, goto, page, locate) and three list buttons (buddy, ignore, blackmark). | The original (tp.exe list handler 0x4BE820) gave 799016 earmuffs on/off, 799017 vote and 799018 the unidentified shared handler 0x4A9380, and the six-button table 6024-6031 (tp.exe 0x758CD8) is a popup on a name in the list (0x4BE290, at the cursor): blackmark, goto, ignore, remove buddy, locate, page (0x4BEDE0); other names get the popup 12290 (blackmark, goto, ignore, add buddy). Still unknown: what 0x4A9380 does and which list flag picks the popup. | `docs/ONLINE.md:360` |
| ONLINE-022 | OpenTPW online extension | The postcard photo attached by Send Postcard is the park's generated map thumbnail, and no park reference is attached. | The original attached a camcorder snapshot of the visited park (manual pp. 51/67); its content and encoding are not decoded. | `docs/ONLINE.md:362` |
| ONLINE-023 | OpenTPW online extension | Park names are masked with the word filter when published, and the stored masked name is what everyone sees. | The original masked at display on the client: the city lobby's park label (tp.exe 0x4B8850), the park details (0x4C0AE0), Find Parks (0x4BFC70) and the other-parks chart (0x4C1E40) pass the name through the filter 0x5D3230 while the filter flag (0x5B09B0) is set, with the local swears.txt/alloweds.txt (0x5AFD70, 0x5D3140). Still unknown: what sets the filter flag (the matching rules are ONLINE-001). | `docs/ONLINE.md:363` |
| ONLINE-024 | OpenTPW online extension | A buddy entering a park is reported as "Your buddy X has entered Y" from OpenTPW's own text. | The original notification's string; CHAT_COMMANDS 111-113 ("Your buddy ") have empty English suffixes and no enter-the-park variant is recovered. | `docs/ONLINE.md:364` |
| ONLINE-076 | OpenTPW online extension | An owner's visits to their own park never count, and one visitor counts at most once per UTC day per park | The original service's guard against repeated or self visits (every visit earned Platinum Tickets, FAQ S2) | `docs/ONLINE.md:365` |
| PATH-001 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | PATH-ENTER: a left click on an object-less cell (hover state 2, help 441) or a path cell (state 1, help 442) enters the path tool (tp.exe click dispatch 0x4879d0 builds the tool object 0x46c580(1), whose enter 0x46d5a0 calls SetMode(1) 0x52f200); the click's release sets that cell as the start only when the start check 0x535670(1, 0) does not refuse it (0x527222..0x5272b8), else the tool stays on without a start; OpenTPW instead refuses to enter when CheckCell fails, the re-preview while game+36 is set (0x5249b6) is not modelled, and the release-time ghost gate (0x524a63) is modelled for clicks after a start (CellBuildTool.Click) but not for the entering click | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:12` |
| PATH-004 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | PATH-CONNECT: the path-to-path links are traced and match (tp.exe 0x5348d0 mode 1: each cardinal neighbour of type 1 gets the back bit and the new cell the forward bit, 0x534976..0x534fb4, directions from the vectors set up at 0x4d9a60); not modelled: the OR into existing link bytes (OpenTPW replaces the cell's own), links to type-9/10 neighbours gated by their +0xd link, the diagonal bits 2/8/0x20/0x80 and their clean-up beside type-3/9 links (0x53518b), the type-3 to type-1 conversion at entry (0x534906) and the queue-neighbour refresh with cat_ui 139 (AUDIO-023) | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:13` |
| PATH-005 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | PATH-REMOVE: the forced path removal (a = b = 0, NoModify respected, no refund) is the original's area clear (tool mode 0x3a from the Delete key handler 0x40c5e0: two clicks span a rectangle and 0x52bdd0 calls ClearCell 0x5367a0(0, 0) on every type-1 cell, 0x52c11f), which Remove matches; OpenTPW's remove tool is one click that clears an object, else a queue cell, else a path cell, while the original's rectangle removes only objects wholly inside it and type-1 path cells and leaves queue and type-10 cells | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:14` |
| PATH-006 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | PATH-SLOPE: no slope or height test, as in the original (tp.exe LayLine 0x536100, SetCell 0x532fc0 reads heights only for the ghost, validator 0x535670 has no float operation, SetCellType 0x5346d0); the object terrain rule OpenTPW applies (water, blocked, entrance area, fixed walkway, holes) stands in for the original's cell types and ownership: map init 0x536490 types only hole cells (2, 0x1e, 7, refused by type) and leaves every other non-InitialPath cell type 0 with the unowned flag 0x40 (PATH-007) | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:15` |
| PATH-007 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | PATH-LAND: no cell gets the unowned flag 0x40 (every in-bounds cell counts as owned); the original sets 0x40 on every non-InitialPath cell at level load (tp.exe 0x536490 from 0x52f050), clears it inside the park fence that 0x5323f0 lays from the level's outline table 0x784e3c anchored on the gate (fill 0x531040/0x5311c0 plus a strip at the gate), refuses 0x40 cells for paths (0x535670) and saves the flags as mFlags (0x4d0b30); OpenTPW decodes neither the outline nor the saved flags, and Buy Land (tool 0x39, 0x5327e0) is not modelled | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:16` |
| PATH-008 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | PATH-CODE8: path over any queue cell is refused; the original's validator returns code 8 for a queue end (one link whose neighbour is a queue; tp.exe 0x535670 at 0x535d00), and code 8 is not a refusal: the start check (0x527280) and the ghost accept it, and the commit's SetCellType (0x5346d0; rule 0x535600 allows type 1 over type 3) turns the queue end into a charged path cell; OpenTPW has no queue-to-path conversion | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:17` |
| PATH-013 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | PATH-PAUSE: while the pause menu is shown the park takes no tool clicks, as in the original (tp.exe gate 0x48c8d0 reads the pause-menu object 0x7c2534 +0xc; the park click handler 0x4879d0 returns before the tool dispatch); OpenTPW's speed pause keeps the tool live; the original's pause key P (0x40bf70 → 0x409350(1)) also toggles a window (0x48a720 → 0x4a27f0 → 0x47ed80): a full-screen layer (0x65dd7b(1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0x7ff, 0x5ff), the 'lolight' backdrop) under the panel 5094 (174,500)-(1874,900) of table 0x752e00, and the park click handler 0x4879d0 does not read the pause flag 0x786b84 | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:18` |
| PATH-014 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | PATH-VARIANT: built straights and edges pick str1/str2 and edg1/edg2 by a hash of the cell (saved cells keep theirs); the original's chooser (tp.exe 0x535dd0) uses the second texture (2 becomes 19, 10 becomes 20) when byte 0x820ac0 is set (0x535fcc), and every call that reaches its end stores rand() & 1 there (CRT rand 0x67b5c0, store 0x53602c, seeded with GetTickCount at 0x54dddc), so each recomputation re-rolls from a shared clock-seeded generator and no per-cell rule exists | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:19` |
| PATH-015 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | QUEUE-TEXTURES: queue cells are drawn with QueueTex ground textures whose shape and turn follow decoded artwork and queue links; the original applies no QueueTex texture: its tile refresh 0x5365d0 sends a queue cell's class-2 result of the chooser 0x535dd0 (table 0x7630b0) to 0x5229e0, which places one of the queue models loaded by 0x522900 (quedead, questra, quebnd2, quebnd1, queend, quebin1, quebin2 .md2) turned by 360 minus the rotation, and gives the ground only the sentinel (0, 8, 0); OpenTPW does not draw these models | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/PathApproximations.cs:20` |
| PTCL-900 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | The key's pose is read from the current animator hierarchy when the EVENT runs; the original reads the key-table matrices refreshed by the object's last draw (tp.exe 0x473C70 -&gt; 0x44AB30, gated by instance-header bit 0x1 and table bit 0x1), so the frozen start position differs from the original's by the animation advanced since the last render and is not stale while the object is culled | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Particles/ParticleApproximations.cs:9` |
| PTCL-904 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | A particle's quad half-height is size / 1024 original units as in tp.exe 0x57C620; the width multiplies it by the frame record's float +8 (textured) or DAT_008BCBCC (untextured, 0x57C71F) in the original, while OpenTPW uses the sprite frame's pixel aspect and 1; DAT_008BCBCC is the display's height / width (0x582EB0 stores param_2 / param_1; 0x5781D0 also doubles it into the screen matrix at 0x87B0D0), so whether OpenTPW's aspect-correct projection makes the untextured factor 1 depends on the space the quads reach the screen matrix in, which is not read; the sprite UVs cover the whole frame | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Particles/ParticleApproximations.cs:10` |
| PTCL-905 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | OpenTPW groups the quads into three blend batches (alpha, source-alpha-over-one, one-plus-one), each in emitter slot order, depth-tested without depth writes and not depth sorted; the original submits one batch per particle (0x565F90 -&gt; 0x565240), slots 0..119 ascending and newest particle first, each with its own state word, which the decoder 0x567620 turns into: 0x1256 opaque (no blend, no alpha test), 0x556 alpha blend with an alpha test at 0x10/255, 0x200522 one-plus-one and 0x200552 source-alpha-over-one with that alpha test, 0x2756 alpha blend with an alpha test at 0xF0/255, all with depth writes unless flags 0x4000 add 0x800; an untextured particle is opaque (0x1256) when its alpha byte is 0 or &gt;= 0xF0, else 0x556 (0x57C6FA-0x57C71B), and flags bit 4 (0x200522, +0x30 for 0x2000) and 0x20000 (MAGFILTER point) modify it; a batch whose state lacks 0x1000 and 0x2000 (the renderer mask 0x3000, 0x5667D0) has its triangles depth-sorted within it (0x565590 -&gt; 0x565490), the others go straight to vtable +0x10, and the order across batches is not read | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Particles/ParticleApproximations.cs:11` |
| PTCL-906 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | The engine position of an EVENT is converted back to native units (world floats * 1024, truncated as the original's _ftol does) by inverting OpenTPW's placement (0.2 engine units per original unit, the park heightfield's centring, Z-up to Y-up); the pool runs with the options' built-in default PARTICLEDENSITY (500) and 2048 particles, the user's saved density value is not read and the option is not exposed, and the terrain-height callback is the original's flat-zero stub | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Particles/ParticleApproximations.cs:12` |
| PTCL-908 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | Wired: EVENT 1/2, ADDOBJ 1/2 (tp.exe 0x551F5F, 0x5522C5) and the per-type effects of the category .sam (Info.CreateParticleEffect, DestroyParticleEffect, RepairParticleEffect, UpgradeParticleEffect): build at the script attach (0x558e7c), demolish at the removal (0x5590f7), repair switched by REPAIREFFECT (0x556DE0) and upgrade when it is installed (0x55A100), each at the footprint middle, the first, second and fourth sized to the footprint (0x520030); the footprint box height (record +0x1C/+0x28) is taken as zero. Not wired: the removal's mode in which the global at +0x1da738 is 2 (0x4dd0a0 passes 0, no demolish effect) is not modelled, an object without a footprint box starts no effect in OpenTPW although 0x559060 creates it anyway (at coordinates the box call leaves unset), ADDOBJ keeps the handle for KILLOBJ but the effect does not follow the key node on later scheduler runs (0x5516B0 calls 0x51FE30 with the node position truncated to whole units), ADDOBJ_EXT (opcode 9, which carries the control operand) has no VM handler, a negative key (the object's box centre, 0x556B90/0x466B70), a scoped effect (bit 0x8000, the per-type slot table at record +0x4E0, 0x4145D0) and an unresolved key start nothing, the further dispatch after the upgrade effect (0x51BFC0) and the scoped message after the build effect (0x51C640), and the remaining engine callers (UI pools 0x5ED5A0, key 0x599880, beams 19/15 0x549C60/0x54AC70/0x546280/0x54AE50, 0x557334/0x557f0e, stink 50 0x4D93B0, smoke 2 0x544C80, splash 96 0x547F50, fly smoke and fire 39/40 0x55DBA3/0x55DC22, thing effects 0x4FA470) | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Particles/ParticleApproximations.cs:13` |

| WALK-005 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | a cell is walkable when it is on the current walk (route and extra open cells), so path cells beside the route are walls; the original commits a step through the callback 0x510510 (at most one cell, diagonal steps through either L-shaped route) and the edge test 0x4d8750 with the guest's mask (walk +0xb4): path-to-path steps are always open, queue boundaries need the target's link back, other cell types and the masks 1 and 2 have their own rules; none of this is modelled | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/WalkApproximations.cs:17` |
| WALK-006 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | a queue walk may also enter the queue cell ahead of its destination and the one behind its start, and no other cell off its route; in the original a step from a queue cell is open (edge test 0x4d8750) into a path, queue or queue-end neighbour whose link points back at it, or into a type-0x1e fixed walkway when the mask is not 2, and blocked otherwise; OpenTPW does not read the cell links for this | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/WalkApproximations.cs:18` |
| WALK-007 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | no map-edit reroute: the original stamps the 16 x 16-cell block of a changed cell with a global counter (0x4d8c50/0x4d8c60, from 0x5346d0, 0x538fc0, 0x5348d0 and 0x4de1f0), and every walk step follow_path (0x50ed10) compares the stamp of the guest's block (0x4d8cd0) with the walk's +72 (0x50ed6c) and searches again (0x50f8e0 mode 1); a failed search sets +184 and ends the walk as arrived; OpenTPW reroutes only on the stuck rule | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/WalkApproximations.cs:19` |
| WALK-008 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | guests on the arrival lanes walk off the path grid, with no wall force and every position committed; the original walks them like every guest (route search 0x50f8e0, commit 0x510510 and edge test 0x4d8750 with the walk mask), the lane cells being type 0x1e or path from map init 0x536490, so only map-edge, entrance-building, water, ride and queue cells could push or refuse them | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/WalkApproximations.cs:20` |
| WALK-009 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | the progress history compares raw remaining distances inside the five-waypoint window and skips the first step after route setup; the original's progress is ONE - trunc(remaining x ONE / +160) (0x50fd40; ONE when arrived or +160 is 0), where +160 is the whole route's octile length from the position at setup or refill, compared (&lt;=) with +172 (0x50f5c7), which setup and every refill reset to 0 together with the history +176 (0x50f8e0) | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/WalkApproximations.cs:21` |
| WALK-010 | Original-fidelity area (scope unadjudicated) | the needs phase that gates the toilet speed term compares the park turn with OpenTPW's guest id; the original compares the low two bits of the tick with the thing id (tp.exe 0x501676), taken from one pool of 10239 slots shared by every thing kind (allocator 0x516270, ids 1, 2, 3, ... on a fresh park, freed ids reused last-in first-out after the turn's deferred destroy), so the phase depends on the whole construction and destroy history | See source annotation/runtime register | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/WalkApproximations.cs:22` |

## Retired and reserved IDs

IDs that a table row in docs keys, or docs prose cites as `APPROX:ID`, but no register declares: retired approximations and IDs a plan reserves. Declaring one of them fails the check, so a new approximation cannot take a number the docs already use with another meaning. `--write` keeps every listed ID; remove an entry by hand only when its docs agree with the new declaration.

| WALK-001 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/RideVisitorBridge.cs:155` | [APPROX:WALK-001] the amount of the special ingredient stays at the ride build's 50 (tp.exe 0x4db090 stores 0x32 at +0x198, 0x4db3b3), so a Sugar visit adds 3 (0x4fe5f8: trunc(6 x amount / 100)); the original's shop window shows a 0..100 amount slider for ingredient shops only (range set at 0x4b06d2; refresh 0x4b0950 hides controls 0xc086/0xc084 unless the type's ingredient is 1..4), writes it to the object (0x4b0aa0) or to every object of that type (0x4b0ae0) and saves it as mAmountOfSpecialIngredient (0x4db7d0); OpenTPW has no slider, its save does not store the amount and the original-save import skips it — still open: no slider or save field in OpenTPW |
| WALK-002 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestNavigation.cs:262` | [APPROX:WALK-002] avoid_walls (tp.exe 0x50e4a0, Mac 0xfdc70) models only its edge branch (the first pushing edge of +x, -x, +y, -y, 2 x the penetration) and takes the cell of the probe; the original takes the cell of the position (0x5104b0 on walk +8/+0xc, 0x50e4eb) and, when no edge pushes, runs one corner block for that cell's corner in the motion quadrant (0x50e75e, 0x50e8b3, 0x50ea07, 0x50eb5e) with the ray test 0x50cc20, which OpenTPW leaves out — still open: the corner branch and the position cell are not ported |

| WALK-004 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestNavigation.cs:182` | [APPROX:WALK-004] routes are OpenTPW's cell sequences (one Grid.TryStep cell per destination, the queue cells in order, Grid.FindPath for the bin and wander walks) that start at the guest's own cell and are refilled five waypoints at a time from the stored route; the stuck reroute prepends the current cell to the rest; the original searches from the guest's cell at every SetDest (0x510100, setup 0x50f8e0, search 0x511420 = line-and-boundary search 0x511470 plus refinement 0x5108a0), again at each refill (0x50efce) and at the stuck reroute (0x50ee79, mode 1, current cell prepended), and ends the walk when a search fails (+0x60 = +0xb8 = 1, 0x50f945) — still open: the search 0x511470 and refinement 0x5108a0 are not ported |
| WALK-005 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestSimulation.cs:677` | [APPROX:WALK-005] a cell is walkable when it is on the current walk (route and extra open cells), so path cells beside the route are walls; the original commits a step through the callback 0x510510 (at most one cell, diagonal steps through either L-shaped route) and the edge test 0x4d8750 with the guest's mask (walk +0xb4): path-to-path steps are always open, queue boundaries need the target's link back, other cell types and the masks 1 and 2 have their own rules; none of this is modelled — still open: the edge test 0x4d8750 on ParkCellMap and the mask writers are not ported |
| WALK-006 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestSimulation.cs:1437` | [APPROX:WALK-006] a queue walk may also enter the queue cell ahead of its destination and the one behind its start, and no other cell off its route; in the original a step from a queue cell is open (edge test 0x4d8750) into a path, queue or queue-end neighbour whose link points back at it, or into a type-0x1e fixed walkway when the mask is not 2, and blocked otherwise; OpenTPW does not read the cell links for this — still open: the edge test 0x4d8750 on the queue links is not ported (WALK-005) |
| WALK-007 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestNavigation.cs:297` | [APPROX:WALK-007] no map-edit reroute: the original stamps the 16 x 16-cell block of a changed cell with a global counter (0x4d8c50/0x4d8c60, from 0x5346d0, 0x538fc0, 0x5348d0 and 0x4de1f0), and every walk step follow_path (0x50ed10) compares the stamp of the guest's block (0x4d8cd0) with the walk's +72 (0x50ed6c) and searches again (0x50f8e0 mode 1); a failed search sets +184 and ends the walk as arrived; OpenTPW reroutes only on the stuck rule — still open: no block stamps in OpenTPW |
| WALK-008 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestNavigation.cs:109` | [APPROX:WALK-008] guests on the arrival lanes walk off the path grid, with no wall force and every position committed; the original walks them like every guest (route search 0x50f8e0, commit 0x510510 and edge test 0x4d8750 with the walk mask), the lane cells being type 0x1e or path from map init 0x536490, so only map-edge, entrance-building, water, ride and queue cells could push or refuse them — still open: lane guests are not walked through the edge test |

| coaster-completion-click | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterBuilder.cs:127` | [EXT:coaster-completion-click] OpenTPW joins a cut circuit only when the closing link passes the validator and no node is marked invalid, as it completes a fresh circuit only then (see Complete): the original's close click tests nothing |
| coaster-completion-click | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterBuilder.cs:226` | [EXT:coaster-completion-click] OpenTPW completes a coaster only when its circuit is closed and no node is marked invalid (flag 0x4000), by a click on a station end after the closing click, or by Enter: a design decision. The original completes on one click on any cell whose type is 4, 9 or 10 (ride and queue-end cells) or whose byte +0xf has bit 0 (cell evaluator default case 0x534584..0x5345d0, click 0x524960 → 0x435570) and the completion appends the end node and sets state 2 without testing the invalid marks or the open bit; a click elsewhere adds a pylon (0x4419b0 → 0x435020), or closes a reopened circuit on its open start. Completing an unclosed or invalid track would hand the sampler and the rating a closing link of any length, which OpenTPW's curve fit does not guard against |
| coaster-ledger | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterBuilder.cs:388` | [EXT:coaster-ledger] track money is posted as other costs and other income (ParkEconomy), which matches the debit and credit totals, and joins the station's total spent, which is OpenTPW's own extension; the debit's withdrawals gate, last balance and turn-entered-red stamp come from ParkEconomy's posting |
| coaster-own-save | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterWorld.cs:28` | [EXT:coaster-own-save] the own-format park save holds the tracks, trains, riders and the coaster generator state (CoasterSave, since the park-resume coaster slice); the original's own coaster chunks between EMAK and SAOC are read structurally only (SaveCoasterBlock: header, auxiliary links, sections, links; train payload raw; nonempty TPW runtime import refused) and never written; importing them is a non-goal until someone needs it (the evidence for it would be the train payload layout in the save writer next to the loader 0x437b60) |
| coaster-resume-reopen | `source/OpenTPW/World/Level.Coasters.cs:322` | [EXT:coaster-resume-reopen] a save taken while the coaster tool was open holds the ride out of service (state 2, its object closed); the tool is not open after a load, so the ride is let out as leaving the tool would (0x447520 → 0x4df390; the original's save during the tool is not traced) |
| coaster-rng-seed | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterWorld.cs:36` | [EXT:coaster-rng-seed] the coaster generator starts from the world seed (folded script stream seed) instead of the original's one C-library rand() at world init (0x457a90 → 0x67b5c0, stored at 0x7b0958); the boarding step itself is the original's s = s·0x343fd + 0x269ec3 with (s &gt;&gt; 16) % n (0x43b67f..0x43b69d): a determinism design decision (DET-I2 owns ClibRng) |
| coaster-save-migration | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:818` | [EXT:coaster-save-migration] OpenTPW's own old-save rule: a track saved before the three-node station keeps its pylons, circuit flags and node marks; the station nodes are the ones the placement built from the station model, the end node of a completed track stands at S0 again, and the controller re-samples the whole circuit (the saved train placement belongs to the old sampling and is dropped by CoasterRide.RestoreSnapshot) |
| coaster-state-clear | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:151` | [EXT:coaster-state-clear] the original does nothing on 4 → 1 and 8 → 1 (cases 4 and 8 of 0x435730 have no state 1 branch); the tool always goes through state 2 first, where 2 → 1 removes the trains, but OpenTPW's headless edits (Reopen, Delete, SetStation) also change an open or closed ride to state 1 and then take the trains off with their riders so none is left on a track that is gone; the power factor reset of 1 → 2 is derived from the ride mode here, and the highlight (0x441840) is not drawn |
| coaster-station-end-click | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:617` | [EXT:coaster-station-end-click] a click on the station's '&lt;' or '&gt;' cell closes the circuit too, as it did when the station's ends were the track's ends; the original's own completion click takes any ride cell (types 4, 9, 10), see CoasterBuilder.Complete |

| track-pool-overflow-refused | `source/OpenTPW/World/Tracks/BumpController.cs:224` | [EXT:track-pool-overflow-refused] a circuit that overflows the section, collision-object or buoy pool is not a ride: the walk's verdict is overridden to invalid and the geometry is dropped, so no boat runs on a partial track; the original skips sections silently when the section pool is full, crashes on a collision-object overflow (it logs "Out of Collision Objects" and writes the flags through a null pointer, [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x0054B1E0]) and drops buoys ("Out of Buoys") |
| track-tile-owner | `source/OpenTPW/World/Tracks/TrackBuilder.cs:61` | [EXT:track-tile-owner] OpenTPW tags every tile with its ride so rides side by side stay separate and a demolition removes the ride's own tiles; the original's 40-byte cell has no owner field and its walk and demolition follow the masks (its vehicles can cross into a neighbour ride's adjacent objects, see TRACK-019) |
| trig-anim-speed-guard | `source/OpenTPW/VM/Handlers/Animation.cs:61` | [EXT:trig-anim-speed-guard] A TRIGANIMSPEED speed operand of 0 or below faults the script: PC 0x552f2e..0x552feb stores the operand at +0xe4 as 16 bits and divides by the full operand (0 is a divide fault there too), a negative speed gives a decreasing frame whose end test in 0x4735d0 never fires, and the sampler's unsigned key searches (0x470c90) would read the 12-byte pose table out of bounds; the corpus has 4 uses, all literals from 1800 to 4000, so this is OpenTPW's guard for malformed input (formerly RIDES-038) |
| ui-anchors | `source/OpenTPW/UI/Original/UiCanvas.cs:48` | [EXT:ui-anchors] anchors keep the authored distance to the nearest edge on non-4:3 outputs; the original (1999) ran fixed 4:3 modes, so widescreen layout is an OpenTPW design decision |
| ui-key-navigation | `source/OpenTPW/UI/Original/UiInput.cs:72` | [EXT:ui-key-navigation] arrows, Enter, Tab and Space navigate and activate the dialogs (none of the original's six key groups, 0x747f38-0x748378, contains them); Escape is the game group's menu key (0x40c180) and the pause key follows KEYBOARD.str string 20 (default P, system row 0, 0x40bf70) |
| upgrade-confirm | `source/OpenTPW/Hud/ParkHud.RideUpgrade.cs:12` | [EXT:upgrade-confirm] the host confirmation for catalogue-supported ordinary ride upgrades has no original rule to match |
| upscaling | `source/OpenTPW/UI/Original/Options/OptionsScreen.cs:49` | [EXT:upscaling] render-scale steps (presets 77/67/59/50 from the display slice plus 5% steps) |
| vm-fault-containment | `source/OpenTPW/VM/RideVM.cs:196` | [EXT:vm-fault-containment] a faulted script's clock stops and its children are removed; the original has no managed |
| vm-fault-containment | `source/OpenTPW/World/Objects/OriginalObjectRuntime.cs:198` | [EXT:vm-fault-containment] a faulted root script stops its object: riders, trains, controller and sounds are released |
| vm-host-time | `source/OpenTPW/VM/RideScriptWorld.cs:43` | [EXT:vm-host-time] the original reads the C runtime's local time on every execution (tp.exe 0x67D3F0/0x67D8E0), so YEAR to SEC are external inputs: managed host-local time here, neither saved nor hashed, while the variable writes and flags that follow are VM state. A replay needs the same samples (an OpenTPW replay design point; the tests and headless runs inject a fixed clock) |
| weather-stream | `source/OpenTPW/World/ParkWeather.cs:28` | [EXT:weather-stream] the weather's random draws (roll, lightning and thunder countdowns) come from the park's own weather stream; the PC draws them from the world LCG 0x516330 (world +0x1da708 = ror13(s * 0x19660D + 0x3C6EF35F), the rotated value stored, \|value\| returned; seeded from time(NULL) at world creation 0x51586b, setter 0x516370), shared by about 139 call sites, so reproducing its draw order needs every user of that LCG (docs/DETERMINISM.md) |
| window-key-toggle | `source/OpenTPW/Hud/ParkHud.ScreenShortcuts.cs:39` | [EXT:window-key-toggle] a second press of a window key closes that window (toggle) and A/S stay with the camera pan; the original closes with Esc and re-opens on a repeat key (0x488ba0, key group "shortcuts" 0x748378), its callbacks sit in the release-side slot (OpenTPW fires on press), and A (allitems) and S (allstaff) are real keys — OpenTPW design decisions, no evidence needed |
| world-capture | `source/OpenTPW/Client/WorldCapture.cs:14` | [EXT:world-capture] developer/screenshot command, not original behaviour |

Up to depth 128 the cell's own link to the front picks the axis: link 1 (−Y) gives (lateral, depth), 4 (+X) (255 − depth, lateral), 16 (+Y) (lateral, 255 − depth), 64 (−X) (depth, lateral). Beyond 128 (the fourth place) the axis is the link of the queue cell behind (0x004DE670), for the back cell the direction from its attached path cell (the first of its connections 1, 4, 16, 64 that leads to a path cell, reversed), and link 4 then gives depth − 128 instead of 255 − depth |

| TPW-EXE:0x4092a0 game pause | `source/OpenTPW/Hud/ParkHud.cs:121` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x4092a0 game pause] the game pause (object 0x786b68, +0x1c = 1) is called by the pause menu 0x48c830 (0x48c87e, from Esc), by the full-screen map window 0x5f0b40 (0x5f0b7f, from Space) and by the postcard 0x4a9380 (0x4a93d7, Ctrl+P), and by the generic dialog 0x47f020 (0x47f26c; probably the message box, medium confidence). Callers 0x46b74c, 0x49efba, 0x49f29f and 0x4a3a6c are unidentified, so the park only pauses here for the screens whose original pauses (the pause menu with the screens opened from it, and the map window) |
| TPW-EXE:0x40BDA0 | `source/OpenTPW/Audio/GameAudio.cs:149` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x10112430 backtrack key] the key command "backtrack" (Backspace, command table data 0x455B0): tool mode 0, mouse-mode state 0 or 1 and a path cell under the cursor play cat_ui 95 at (x·10, 0, y·10) before the single-cell removal (mode 0x32, build pass 0x10070B98, ClearCell's path case); [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40BDA0] the same, its sound call at 0x40BE71 |
| TPW-EXE:0x40BDA0 backtrack key | `source/OpenTPW/World/ParkPathBuilder.cs:348` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40BDA0 backtrack key] with a tool object on (vtable 0x6FE9E0, slot +0x24 returns 3) Backspace calls Back 0x52FE50(0, 1) at 0x40BEB3, which pops the vertex stack (count 0x820A8C), switches to mode 0x34 and clears the line from the popped vertex to the new top (0x525F71: LayLine pass 0x32, ClearCell 0x5367A0 with the walk step, so each cell's placement counter drops by one and the cell goes once it falls below 0; the path case spends nothing), then continues from the new top; with only the start left it changes nothing |
| TPW-EXE:0x40BE8B backtrack click | `source/OpenTPW/World/Level.Objects.cs:322` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40BE8B backtrack click] the key handler clicks the cell in mode 0x32, whose one-cell LayLine (0x536100) calls SetCell(0x32, 0, 1) and so ClearCell 0x5367A0 with (0, 1): the counted removal, not the forced one |
| TPW-EXE:0x40C180 Escape in a tool | `source/OpenTPW/Client/ThemeParkIncHud.cs:74` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40C180 Escape in a tool] Escape ends a tool (installs the default park mode, SetMode(0)) and is consumed; only an Escape with no tool on reaches the pause menu row (0x748378, 0x40C4D0) |
| TPW-EXE:0x40c120 turn key ',' | `source/OpenTPW/Hud/ParkHud.ScreenShortcuts.cs:268` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40c120 turn key ','] game key group row 1 (0x74803c) sets the build angle (0x81d7a4, getter 0x52f1f0, setter 0x52f1b0) to (angle + 0x5a) mod 0x168; [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40c150 turn key '.'] row 2 (0x748050) adds 0x10e. The angle goes unchanged to the placement check and commit 0x528a70 (0x524c51..0x524c63), as Level.BuildRotation does to PlaceObject (ObjectFootprint: rotation 90 maps (u, v) to (X+v, Y-u), verified on Easymode saves) |
| TPW-EXE:0x40c150 turn key '.' | `source/OpenTPW/Hud/ParkHud.ScreenShortcuts.cs:268` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40c120 turn key ','] game key group row 1 (0x74803c) sets the build angle (0x81d7a4, getter 0x52f1f0, setter 0x52f1b0) to (angle + 0x5a) mod 0x168; [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40c150 turn key '.'] row 2 (0x748050) adds 0x10e. The angle goes unchanged to the placement check and commit 0x528a70 (0x524c51..0x524c63), as Level.BuildRotation does to PlaceObject (ObjectFootprint: rotation 90 maps (u, v) to (X+v, Y-u), verified on Easymode saves) |
| TPW-EXE:0x40c3b0 speed actions | `source/OpenTPW/Economy/ParkCalendar.cs:13` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40c3b0 speed actions] the key table at 0x7480a0 maps VK_SUBTRACT (0x6D) to 0x40c3b0 (rate × 0.8, 0x403340), VK_ADD (0x6B) to 0x40c3c0 (rate × 1.25, 0x4032f0) and VK_MULTIPLY (0x6A) to 0x40c3d0 (rate 1.0, 0x4032d0); both steps clamp to 0.25..2.0 (0x6fd648 / 0x6fd650) on the clock 0x785970 +0x20 |
| TPW-EXE:0x40c5d0 system key row 1 'toghelp' | `source/OpenTPW/Hud/ParkHud.Help.cs:8` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40c5d0 system key row 1 'toghelp'] Ctrl+H (0x4817f0) shows or hides the global help object (0x77c48c) and stores the new state in options byte 0x78d90e = options +0x36, the Popup help (TooltipsOn) option the options screen edits; the help box geometry is UI-010 |
| TPW-EXE:0x40cb80 key group initialiser, 0x40caa0 helper | `source/OpenTPW/Global/Input.Localized.cs:12` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x40cb80 key group initialiser, 0x40caa0 helper] the 21 strings of table 0x3fc are copied into the char byte (+2) of the shortcut rows 1..11 (strings 0..10), the camcorder row 16 (string 11), the staff locator, visitor locator and postcard rows (12..14), the game rows Ctrl+C and Ctrl+O (16, 17) and ',' and '.' (18, 19), and the system rows pause (20) and toghelp (15); Space (map) and Esc are never overridden. For example Dutch buy is K, hire is A and research is O |
| TPW-EXE:0x419710 font banks | `source/OpenTPW/UI/Original/UiCanvas.cs:43` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x419710 font banks] the original loads one bank of 13 font slots by screen mode: Res.RESOLUTION [0x7858ec] 1, 2, 3, 4+ (512×384, 640×480, 800×600, 1024×768 and up) or else the options resolution index [0x78d8dc] 0, 1, 2, 3+ give bank 0, 1, 2, 3 (MENU/TITLE/SESH/CASH SMALL, MED, BIG, BIG) |
| TPW-EXE:0x42a190 ride exit | `source/OpenTPW/World/Level.RideView.cs:41` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42a190 ride exit] the exit clears the seat node's mark and, when the camera mode toggled off (0x42a560), takes the cell under the park camera's target and looks for a thing of type 3 there (0x42a340); if there is one the park camera's target moves to the centre of that thing's cell (0x42a1d0: (index - 1 & 0x7f) * 10 + 5 and (index - 1 &gt;&gt; 7) * 10 + 5), otherwise it stays; the camcorder walk (0x42ae70) instead restores the saved target and yaw exactly |
| TPW-EXE:0x42a560 ride camera | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCarRig.cs:143` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42a560 ride camera] a coaster's ride camera takes the node the seat lookup 0x44b220(model, 0x1000, 1) finds in the car model (coasta/megacost cart, minecart cart node 5 "camera") |
| TPW-EXE:0x42a560 ride camera | `source/OpenTPW/World/RideCameraMode.cs:190` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42a560 ride camera] a ride is rideable when its model has the seat record the camera looks up; about 40 ride models carry one (coasters, the Totem, bumper cars, go-karts, tv simulator, rocket and others) |
| TPW-EXE:0x42a560 ride camera | `source/OpenTPW/World/RideSeat.cs:18` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42a560 ride camera] the ride camera looks the seat up with 0x44b220(model, 0x1000, n) and keeps the node's matrix pointer (0x790a30) as the camera frame; the ride WADs carry such records, mostly type 0x1031 value 1 |
| TPW-EXE:0x42b1c0 camera update | `source/OpenTPW/World/RideCameraMode.cs:12` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42b1c0 camera update] the per-frame camera update: with the ride flag set, the camera matrix is the seat node's world matrix (0x790a30) with the three rotation rows blended with the node's matrix of the previous frame (0x790948) and the translation copied exactly (0x42ca04-0x42cb96), then the free-look rotation is multiplied in (0x42cba0-0x42cbdf) |
| TPW-EXE:0x42b8c7 ride look | `source/OpenTPW/World/RideCameraMode.cs:119` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42b8c7 ride look] with the camera flags in 0x3c: x = (pointer x - 0x7a1c54) / 0x74d104 and y = -(pointer y - 0x7a1c58) / 0x74d108 (the view's centre and half size, written at 0x48a14e-0x48a1c5); 0x790ae0 = (x -/+ 0.2) * 3.5 outside +/-0.2 else 0; 0x790adc = (0.2 - y) * 1.2 above 0.2, -y - 0.4 below -0.4, else 0; both are read at 0x42cba5/0x42cbaa and multiplied in as a rotation about X by 0x790adc then about Y by 0x790ae0 (the axis string "XY" at 0x74daa4, 0x46f6f0); the pointer position itself is the input, with no easing of its own |
| TPW-EXE:0x42cba0 view smoothing | `source/OpenTPW/World/RideCameraMode.cs:108` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42cba0 view smoothing] 0x42ca88-0x42cb0b: dt = (current millisecond tick + 1) - the last frame's tick (0x42b1c0), clamped to [1, 1000], a = (1000 - dt) * 0.0005 (0x6fde34 is 0.0005), and every one of the three rotation rows is node * (1 - a) + previousNode * a; the previous matrix (0x790948) is then overwritten with the node's matrix, not with the blend, so the smoothing is a one-frame average, not an accumulating ease |
| TPW-EXE:0x42d5a0 cross section setup | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCrossSections.cs:31` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42d5a0 cross section setup] the loader copies the point blocks as five floats (X, Y, U, Nx, Ny) and adds the definition's fTrackHeightAdjust to every Y (0x42d6d0), then builds the edge blocks (0x42d130), the sections and the selects (0x42e200); the block counts are read from the settings record |
| TPW-EXE:0x42ef05 loft keys | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterDefinition.cs:403` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42ef05 loft keys] pylon +0xd0 = fLoftAnimStartCap, +0xd4 = fLoftAnimEndCap, +0xd8 = fLoftAnimMultiplier, +0xdc = fLoftAnimAdd, each copied from the settings (+0x3914..+0x3920) when nonzero; no PC coaster.sam sets them, so the defaults 0, 1, 1, 0 stand |
| TPW-EXE:0x42f1a0 car type setup | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCarRig.cs:12` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42f1a0 car type setup] each car type record (0x34 bytes) holds the bend flag, the unique id, the mesh name, fFrontDistanceAdd (+0x10), fRearDistanceAdd (+0x14), fYOffset (+0x18) and a default 0.1 (+0x1c, the wheel animation speed factor); the train's front, centre and rear type indices select among them (definition +0x2fc, +0x300, +0x304) |
| TPW-EXE:0x42f717 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterDefinition.cs:139` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x1002FFB0 entrance interval] definition +796 ← settings 15220, unconditional; [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42f717] definition +0x31c ← settings +0x3b74, unconditional; read only by the station window 0x43b750 |
| TPW-EXE:0x42fad0 car spacing | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCarRig.cs:149` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42fad0 car spacing] the loader sums the rear extent of the car before and the front extent of the car itself between neighbouring cars (definition +0x30c..+0x314 for front, centre and rear pairs) and adds one such distance per car to the train length |
| TPW-EXE:0x42fad0 coaster model load | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterPylonRig.cs:14` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42fad0 coaster model load] the TrackMaster loader opens the pylon models with the relative-animation flag 0x1013405 (the only caller to do so, with "GTexture" as the extra texture directory): a vertex key or position key is added to the stored mesh or node, not set |
| TPW-EXE:0x42fad0 train length | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCarRig.cs:68` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x42fad0 train length] the loader walks the cars 0..n−1 and takes the front car type for car 0, the rear car type for car n−1 and the centre car type for the others |
| TPW-EXE:0x430130 car extents | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCarRig.cs:13` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430130 car extents] a car type's front extent (+0x24) is fFrontDistanceAdd plus the model bounds' maximum Z minus the root node's Z, its rear extent (+0x28) fRearDistanceAdd plus the root node's Z minus the minimum Z; the seat count (+0x30) is the number of head sockets of the model ("Car type %s can receive %d heads") |
| TPW-EXE:0x430130 car heads | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRenderer.cs:183` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430130 car heads] each car type counts the head sockets of its model ("Car type %s can receive %d heads"); the riders of a car take them in order |
| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon capabilities | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterAssets.cs:132` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon capabilities] the model's animations by type: loft L, rotate R or R1, incline I, bank B and the second rotate clip R2 |
| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon capabilities | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterDefinition.cs:39` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon capabilities] pylon flags 0x20 (loft clip), 0x40 (rotate), 0x80 (incline), 0x100 (bank) and 0x200 (second rotate clip) are set when the model has the animation of that type (0x472d10); the drag handlers test them (0x441a00, 0x441e80, 0x442010, 0x441cd0) |
| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon clips | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:1130` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon clips] a pylon's animations by type: 3 loft (pylon flag 0x20), 10 rotate (0x40), 2 incline (0x80), 9 bank (0x100), 10 second (0x200); the pylon pose feeds node +0xE0 (yaw), +0xDC (incline) and +0xD8 (bank) to them and the track tag's pose is the knot's direction |
| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon heights | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterPylonRig.cs:65` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon heights] h0 = the track dummy's Y with the loft clip at clamp(fLoftAnimAdd, StartCap, EndCap) (0x430923..0x430990), h1 = its Y at clamp(fLoftAnimMultiplier + fLoftAnimAdd, StartCap, EndCap) (0x430a0b..0x430a92), the stack offset the stack dummy's Y at the first pose minus h0, or 0 without a stack dummy (0x4309be..0x430a01) |
| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon model tags | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterPylonRig.cs:15` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon model tags] the loft, rotate, incline and bank clips and the track and stack dummies are looked up on the loaded model by node attribute (0x2, 0x3, 0x9, 0xa, 0x1000000, 0x800000, 0x2000000, 0x100000, 0x80000) when the control is set up (0x42eb70) |
| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon track dummy | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterPylonRig.cs:59` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon track dummy] the track dummy is the dummy numbered by the first attribute record with bit 0x800000 (0x4306be..0x4306e9: record index +0x2c, dummy number − 1 at +0x30), the stack dummy the one of the first record with 0x100000 (0x430780..0x4307a6); the numbers count the model's dummy nodes from 1 in file order; a model without such a record falls back to the first dummy named Track* or animated by the loft clip |
| TPW-EXE:0x4309a4 pylon heights | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterDefinition.cs:283` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x4309a4 pylon heights] pylon +0xbc = h0, +0xc0 = h1, +0x68 (fMinHeight) = max(fMinHeight, h0) (0x4309a4..0x4309bb), +0xc4 = stack dummy Y − h0 or 0 |
| TPW-EXE:0x4310c8 auto-bank | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:1070` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x4310c8 auto-bank] a node banks unless it carries node flag 0x4 (or 0x100 forces bank 0) and only when it has a next node in the list (the test of the raw link, not the flag-masked neighbour); the PC build path never sets 0x4 and the station end node's 0x144 are creation parameters (0x434060) |
| TPW-EXE:0x431677 node neighbours | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:1054` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x431677 node neighbours] the tangent routine drops the previous neighbour on node flag 0x80000 and the next on 0x40000, and on 0x100000 takes the head node's next node (S1): S0 leaves along S1 − S0, S2 along S2 − S1 whatever the first pylon, and the station end node along S1 − S0 as well |
| TPW-EXE:0x433280 stacked height | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:941` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x433280 stacked height] the height of a stacked node is the sum over the nodes below it of (h0 + t(h1 − h0) + stack offset) of the node's own pylon, then its own h0 + t(h1 − h0): each level adds the pylon's stack offset (+0xc4) |
| TPW-EXE:0x434060 node creation parameters | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:211` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434060 node creation parameters] the constructor maps its parameter bits to node flags: 8 → 0x200, 0x10 → 0x400, 0x20 → 0x1800, 0x40 → 0x80000, 0x80 → 0x40000, 0x100 → 0x100000 (0x4343a6..0x4343eb); bit 0x200 puts the pylon in its map cell (0x452d30) |
| TPW-EXE:0x434060 node creation parameters | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:239` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434060 node creation parameters] bit 4 of the creation parameters goes to 0x433bd0, which skips the fMinHeight/fMaxHeight clamp of 0x433790; the loft parameter is then clamp((h − h0)/(h1 − h0), 0, 1) |
| TPW-EXE:0x434060 node creation parameters | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:643` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434060 node creation parameters] the constructor maps its parameter bits to node flags: 2 → 0x2000, 8 → 0x200, 0x10 → 0x400, 0x20 → 0x1800, 0x40 → 0x80000, 0x80 → 0x40000, 0x100 → 0x100000 (0x4343a6..0x4343eb), bit 0 clear → 0x200000 on the node and its predecessor, bit 4 goes to 0x433bd0; the end node's 0x144 is therefore 0x80000 \| 0x100000, no loop flag 0x40, no 0x4 and no 0x100. The Mac call site 0x10037E34 passes the same 0x144; its node constructor could not be compared (no Mac binary here) |
| TPW-EXE:0x434060 pylon-less knot | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:929` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434060 pylon-less knot] a pylon-less node's knot is its descriptor's exact position (node +0x4c..+0x54); the station end node's descriptor holds the world position of S0's pylon track dummy (0x435570), so the end node stands on the knot of S0 |
| TPW-EXE:0x434153 loop pair | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:457` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434153 loop pair] a node whose pylon control has flag 0x4000 gets node flags \|0xe0 and loop rotate (+0xf8) 0 when it is the first of the pair, \|0x60 and 0.5 when it is the second (the pair counter 0x79c664 is 2, then 1); a trough pylon (0x8000) sets neither |
| TPW-EXE:0x4349a5 distance waiver | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:292` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x4349a5 distance waiver] a link outside [min², max²] is rejected when the previous node's ordinal ([ebp]) is 2 and otherwise waived when its descriptor has flag 2 (0x4349af..0x4349b9): the waiver exempts the links that leave the station's internal nodes S0 and S1 (ordinals 0 and 1, created by 0x436ac0), which OpenTPW never validates; every link after S2 (ordinal 2) and after a pylon is checked (COAST-007 retired, COAST-029) |
| TPW-EXE:0x434d34 stacking on station nodes | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:362` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434d34 stacking on station nodes] the stacking test reads node flags 0x200 and 0x400 of the bottom node of the stack (0x434d34..0x434d3d, 0x434da8..0x434db1), which only the station nodes carry (creation parameters 8 and 0x10): S0 (0x400), S2 (0x200) and S1 (both); no pylon stacks on a station pylon, a placement there is refused as a full stack |
| TPW-EXE:0x434f10 placement area | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterToolPanel.cs:190` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434f10 placement area] the box around the reference node (the last node, the node before the cut) has the half size trunc(sqrt(pylon +0xa0)) + 1 = the maximum place distance + 1 cells, clamped to the map; its upper edges are exclusive (0x5240c0..0x524470) |
| TPW-EXE:0x435020 place debit | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterBuilder.cs:112` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435020 place debit] the PC coaster click path (0x524960 → 0x4419b0 → 0x435020) reads the balance (0x6ad810) and ignores it, then debits ftol(cost) through 0x4d01f0 when money is on (0x785a2c == 0): there is no balance comparison on the path, so nothing here refuses a pylon for money |
| TPW-EXE:0x435570 completion | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:149` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435570 completion] completion sets the track's complete flag, appends the end node and sets state 2 (0x435730); close-circuit (0x442c70) and load (0x437b60) set 2 as well, the cut (0x446740) sets 1 from 2 |
| TPW-EXE:0x435570 completion | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:652` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435570 completion] with definition flag 0x20 one end node is built: a pylon-less descriptor (0x13) at the world position of the first station node's pylon track dummy (head node +0x100 → +0x18 → +0x2c, instance +0x30..+0x38 through 0x435710), creation parameters 0x144 = node flags 0x80000 \| 0x100000 with bit 4; then controller flags 0x1001 and the state setter 0x435730 with state 2; its tangent has no previous neighbour and its next is S1 |
| TPW-EXE:0x435730 state setter | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:147` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435730 state setter] the transitions: 1 → 2 sets the power factor to 1 (outside mode 64), sets every train slot up (0x43be10) and clears the highlight; 1 → 8 and 2 → 8 run the closing move (0x43b390); 2 → 1 removes every train (0x43bf20), clears the shown-trains flag 0x800 and highlights the chain start; 4 → 2 unloads both seat lists of every train (0x43b480 with 0 and 1); 4 → 8 runs the closing move; 8 → 2 unloads both lists and then runs the closing move; every other pair changes nothing |
| TPW-EXE:0x43587f admission on 4 → 2 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:150` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43587f admission on 4 → 2] 4 → 2 and 8 → 2 unload both seat lists and then fall through to the closing move 0x43b390 (0x43587f / 0x4358c9 → 0x4358cb), which moves the guests of the admission ring to the leaving ring: no guest waits on a ride that does not run |
| TPW-EXE:0x435944 mode setter | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:538` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435944 mode setter] from mode 64 to 16 or 32 the power factor becomes 1 and every train gets flag 0x80 and loses 0x40 (0x435947..0x435950); entering mode 64 sets the power factor to 0 (0x4359d5) |
| TPW-EXE:0x435ac9 controller tick | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:710` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435ac9 controller tick] the motion tick runs when state & 0xE (2 built, 4 open, 8 closed); the station step (0x43b8b0, then 0x439570) only when state & 0xC and mode is not 0x40 (0x435cd1, 0x435cd5); the motion tick has no zero-speed branch, so a train in the station keeps the winch floor's speed |
| TPW-EXE:0x435adf scroll shift | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRenderer.cs:24` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435adf scroll shift] the per-frame tick shifts V by δ = (now − ctl+0x138)·P·0.001 for every polygon of a texture the loader flagged (definition flag 4, set when any fScrollRate is nonzero; texture flag 0x20), P the power factor at controller +0x2c; the stored fScrollRate (texture +0xc) is not read in that loop |

| TPW-EXE:0x782f40 pitch ratio tables | `source/OpenTPW/Audio/AudioMixer.cs:69` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x782f40 pitch ratio tables] each table's entry k is the float of 2^((k + 1) / 96) (or its inverse) written with six decimals, so it can differ from the exact power by a few ULP (all 110 entries of both tables match that rule; the test pins some of them bit for bit); the index is one step off: step 1 reads entry 1, 2^(2/96), and 2^(1/96) (entry 0) is never used |
